## Document 007: 10月经济数据跟踪：经济结构分化持续，政策有待逐步落地

Source PDF: all report pdf/10月经济数据跟踪：经济结构分化持续，政策有待逐步落地.pdf
Report source: 万联证券
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 12
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

[Table_RightTitle] 
宏观研究|宏观跟踪报告

[Table_Title] 
经济结构分化持续，政策有待逐步落地

[Table_ReportType] 
——10月经济数据跟踪

[Table_ReportDate] 2026年11月16日

[Table_Summary] ⚫ 事件：2026年10月，工业增加值当月同比增速回落 0.7个百分
点至4.8%，环比上涨0.21%。固投累计同比由-7.2%降至-8.1%，
制造业、基建、房地产投资分别由-2.1%、-4.2%、-20.6%下降为-
2.9%、-5.1%、-21.6%。社零当月同比为0.4%，前值为 1.1%。 
⚫ 工业生产回落，高技术产业再度上行。随十一、中秋节前“抢生
产”效应结束，工业生产季节性回落。产销率同比放缓，发电量
同比持续下滑，工业企业出口保持旺盛。采矿业、制造业增速放
缓，高技术产业再度上行至高位，为工业生产的重要支撑。上游
原材料行业生成整体边际走弱，能化产业链受油价影响增速放
缓，中游设备制造业生产韧性仍强，下游汽车生产持续下跌，AI
相关产品生产保持旺盛，消费品制造业增速整体上行。服务业生
产、商务活动指数回落，服务业活动预期指数小幅下滑。 
⚫ 投资分项全面下滑，地产投资拖累持续。地产投资增速持续下
滑，新开工增速小幅反弹，施工、竣工增速跌幅加深。地产销售
量价齐跌，去库进程持续，二手房销售保持旺盛。三四线城市新
建住宅价格持续承压，房价尚未企稳，房地产“银十”行情表现
偏弱，政策对市场提振效果有限。房企资金来源持续收紧，自筹
资金和国内贷款渠道延续承压，二手房销售给按揭贷款带来支
撑。上月政府集中推地带动下，房企拿地好转。但去库周期和预
售资金制度改革影响，房企资金周转约束持续，拿地进程预期持
续偏缓。 
⚫ 基建增速持续放缓，资金形成实物工作量相对滞后。10月基建投
资下行斜率变陡，随着专项债发行、新型政策性金融工具投放完
成，资金投放进程边际提速，但财政资金拨付到形成实物工作量
仍需时间，未来仍有充足的改善动能。 
⚫ 制造业投资增速回落，高技术产业增速放缓，出口拉动仍强。传
统行业盈利偏弱，制约了企业自主扩产意愿。海外需求保持韧
性，带动了相关行业需求。设备工器具购置同比增速由 9.7%回落
为 8.9%，大规模设备更新政策资金在 7月下达完毕，随着资金逐
步落地，政策效果边际减弱。 
⚫ 消费增速下跌，内需依然偏弱。假期出行前置，部分消费需求提
前释放给10月消费带来一定透支作用，高基数效应和价格波动抑
制了消费意愿。餐饮、商品零售均回落，网上零售增速下滑。可
选消费多数回落，石油及制品类上行幅度最大，通讯器材类跌幅
相对最大，金银珠宝类同比增速回落，金价10月继续下跌，消费
者观望情绪影响下，推迟了购买决策，中秋假期错位至 9月也使
得部分需求提前透支。地产下游行业中，家电和家具类增速小幅
上行，主要受低基数拉动，建材增速继续下滑。必选消费集体走
弱，其中，烟酒类跌幅较大，9月提前备货带来一定影响。

事件 
2026年10月，工业增加值当月同比增速回落0.7个百分点至4.8%（市场预期5.3%），环
比上涨0.21%，前值为0.61%。1-10月，固定资产投资累计同比增速-8.1%（市场预期
-6.9%），前值-7.2%，其中，房地产开发投资累计同比增速回落1个百分点至-21.6%，
基建投资累计同比增速回落0.9个百分点至-5.1%，制造业投资增速累计同比回落0.8
个百分点至-2.9%。社会消费品零售总额当月同比增速由上月的1.1%回落为0.4%（市
场预期1.1%）。

1    工业生产回落，结构分化依然显著 
工业生产季节性回落，下滑幅度超出市场预期，产销率同比放缓，发电量同比持续下
滑，工业企业出口仍有支撑。10月，工业增加值当月同比增速回落0.7个百分点至4.8%，
低于市场预期的5.3%，环比上涨0.21%，前值为0.61%。随十一、中秋节前“抢生产”
效应结束，工业生产季节性回落。发电量同比由上月的-1.2%回落至-2.1%。工业企业
出口交货值当月同比由上月的17%继续上行至18.6%，海外制造业景气度较高，价格上
涨，原油、AI产业链拉动下，出口仍是工业生产重要拉动项。规模以上工业企业产销
率当月同比由-0.2%下行为-0.4%。 
采矿业、制造业增速放缓，高技术产业再度上行至高位，为工业生产的重要支撑。分
大类看，10月，采矿业同比回落0.6个百分点至0.7%，石油和天然气开采业同比高位
小幅放缓，高油价部分压制了石化产业链生产。制造业同比由上月的6%回落至5.4%；
高技术产业同比由上月的15.3%上行至17.6%，装备制造业同比增速为10.6%，持续领
跑制造业整体表现，传统制造业生产相对偏弱。电力、燃气及水的生产和供应业同比
回落1.2个百分点至4.4%。

水泥产量同比跌幅收窄，地产对上游产业链拖累作用持续。有色金属冶炼及压延工业
同比跌幅收窄。能化产业链来看，化学原料及化学制品制造业同比由2.4%回落至1.3%，
乙烯产量同比回落，原油价格高位给产业链带来的影响预期仍将持续；化学纤维产量
同比跌幅扩大，下游纺织业增速同比回落1.2个百分点至2.0%。制造业中游生产整体
保持韧性，通用设备、电气机械及器材制造业增速与上月持平，专用设备制造业同比
边际放缓，整体生产处于高位。下游制造业中，汽车制造业同比大幅下跌5.8个百分
点至0.9%，本月跌幅相对最大，汽车产量同比跌幅加深，SUV产量下跌为主要拖累。
计算机、通信和其他电子设备制造业同比由16.4%上行至19.4%，本月上行幅度相对最
大，集成电路产量同比由18.4%上行至21.9%，为电子计算机产量同比跌幅加深，工业
机器人产量同比走高。铁路、船舶、航空航天和其他运输设备制造业同比回落2.5个
百分点至9.2%。消费品制造业中，医药制造业同比上行1个百分点至5.9%，食品制造
业增速上行，农副食品加工业增速小幅回落。

2    投资：固投分项全面下滑，设备购置增速回落 
固定资产投资分项全部下滑，地产、基建、制造业增速同步回落，资金向实物工作量
转化滞后，企业扩产意愿不足。2026年10月，固定资产投资累计同比增速-8.1%，市
场预期-6.9%，前值-7.2%，固投季调环比增速为-2.93%，前值为0.85%。其中，房地
产开发投资累计同比增速回落1个百分点至-21.6%，增速持续下滑；基建累计同比增
速回落0.9个百分点至-5.1%，制造业累计同比增速回落0.8个百分点至-2.9%，民间固
定资产投资回落1个百分点至-11.4%。第一产业固定投资累计同比下滑幅度最大，第
二、三产业同比跌幅持续加深。设备工器具购置同比增速由9.7%回落为8.9%；建筑安
装工程同比由-10%回落为-11%，新开工偏弱的影响持续。

自2026年10月1日起实施，优惠政策主要针对刚需住房，房地产“银十”行情表现偏
弱，政策对市场提振效果有限。居民消费信心偏弱，加杠杆仍然偏谨慎，预期年内地
产销售延续低位。

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

房企资金来源持续收紧，自筹资金和国内贷款渠道延续承压，二手房销售给按揭贷款
带来支撑。房地产开发资金来源增速由-21.6%进一步回落至-21.9%。其中，自筹资金
和国内贷款延续下行，房企资质偏弱影响下，相关资金来源受阻。定金和预收款同比
由-14.8%回落至-15.5%，新房销售偏弱影响持续，预售资金也受政策调整影响。个人
按揭贷款同比持平于-22.1%，二手房销售交易活跃及前期按揭滞后发放带来支撑。 
上月政府集中推地带动下，房企拿地好转。100大中城市土地成交来看，10月房企拿
地同环比增速均反弹上行，地方政府为完成土地出让收入目标，百城土地供应量在9
月集中放量，企业拿地存在一定滞后效应，上月供应增加在本月成交端兑现。而百城
土地供应量在10月同、环比大幅回落，月度供应量波动仍大，房地产行业去库存进程
下，叠加预售资金制度改革影响，房企资金周转约束持续，房企拿地进程预期持续偏
缓。

2.2  基建增速持续放缓，资金形成实物工作量相对滞后 
基建投资增速下行斜率变陡，资金形成实物工作量相对滞后。10月基建累计同比增速
回落0.9个百分点至-5.1%，下行速率变陡。随着专项债发行、新型政策性金融工具投
放完成，资金投放进程边际提速，但财政资金拨付到形成实物工作量仍需时间，未来
仍有充足的改善动能。 
基建各分项增速集体回落，水利、环境、公共设施管理业跌幅较大。从细分项来看，
电力、热力、燃气及水的生产和供应业基数逐步走低，但跌幅进一步加深。交通运输、
仓储、邮政业增速由1.9%下跌至0.7%，依然保持正增；水利、环境、公共设施管理业
增速下行1.6个百分点至-14.4%，跌幅相对最大。

图表12：基建投资回落，水泥产量下滑（%） 图表13：水利、环境和公共设施管理业跌幅较大 (%)

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

2.3  制造业增速回落，高技术产业仍为重要支撑 
制造业投资增速放缓，民间投资持续下跌，高技术产业增速放缓，出口拉动仍强。制
造业累计同比增速回落0.8个百分点至-2.9%，民间固定资产投资回落1个百分点至-
11.4%。高技术产业投资累计同比由4.9%回落至4%，增速逐步放缓，但依然为制造业
投资主要拉动项。传统行业盈利偏弱，制约了企业自主扩产意愿。海外需求保持韧性，
价格走高和AI产业链为主要拉动，也带动了相关行业投资分化。设备工器具购置同比
增速由9.7%回落为8.9%，大规模设备更新政策资金在7月下达完毕，随着资金逐步落
地，政策效果边际减弱。后续随着财政资金逐步落地形成实物工作量，有望给制造业
投资带来支撑。

### Page 9

3    消费增速再度放缓，服务、商品消费均回落 
消费增速再度放缓，内需依然偏弱，餐饮、商品零售均回落，网上零售增速下滑。10
月，社会消费品零售总额同比增速由上月的1.1%回落为0.4%，市场预期1.1%。假期出
行前置，部分消费需求提前释放给10月消费带来一定透支作用，高基数效应和价格波
动抑制了消费意愿。其中，除汽车以外的消费品零售增速由上月的3.9%下跌至2.5%；
限额以上企业零售同比由上月的-2.1%下跌至-4.9%，大企业经营依然相对承压。餐饮
同比由上月的2.1%下跌至0.7%；商品零售由1%降至0.3%。城镇消费同比增速由0.8%降
至0.2%，乡村消费同比增速由2.7%降至1.5%。商品网上零售累计同比增速由3.8%降至
2.9%，网上商品和服务零售占社零比重逐步回落。从更新到9月的消费者信心数据来
看，消费者信心、收入指数继续回落，消费意愿指数回落幅度相对最大。

图表16：消费增速再度下滑（%） 图表17：网上零售和服务占社零比重逐步回落（%）

资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所 资料来源：Wind，万联证券研究所

弱，价格上涨给消费形成压制，消费补贴政策透支也带来一定影响。金银珠宝类同比
增速由-9.6%大幅下行至-28.7%，金价10月继续下跌，消费者观望情绪影响下，推迟
了购买决策，中秋假期错位至9月也使得部分需求提前透支。双十一活动在10月开启，
化妆品增速持续放缓，结合线上消费数据来看，对线上消费拉动效果有限。汽车消费
由-21.2%回落至-21.4%，依然表现偏弱。地产下游行业中，家电和家具类增速小幅上
行，主要受低基数拉动，建材增速继续下滑。必选消费集体走弱，其中，烟酒类跌幅
较大，由13.8%下滑至2.5%，9月提前备货带来一定影响；粮油、食品、饮料、中西药
品类增速均放缓，日用品、服饰类增速转负。

4    总结与展望 
经济结构分化持续，政策有待进一步落地。10月经济数据整体回落，结构分化持续，
新经济仍是主要拉动，旧经济动能依然偏弱。财政资金投放边际提速，但到位进程依
然偏缓，给经济的支撑效果相对有限。随着政策加速落地形成实物工作量，“六张网”
投资有望逐步上行，推动基建投资修复。年内经济结构分化的特征有望持续，经济有
望逐步企稳，完成全年经济增长目标压力不大。

居民购房、消费持续偏弱，地缘政治风险发展超预期，政策推进和落地不及预期。

## Document 038: 2026-11-16_［QE解説］7～9月期実質GDPは年率▲0.1％とほぼ横ばいの結果（1次QE）_714aa7e4

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026-11-16_［QE解説］7～9月期実質GDPは年率▲0.1％とほぼ横ばいの結果（1次QE）_714aa7e4.pdf
Report source: Mizuho
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

1

7～9 月期実質GDP は年率▲0.1％と 
ほぼ横ばいの結果（1 次QE）

○ 2026年7～9月期の実質GDP（1次速報値）は前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）と、ほぼ横ばいの結果。
4～6月期（前期比＋0.4％）から減速し、経済の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服 
○ 個人消費が2四半期連続でマイナスとなったほか、輸出が一時的要因もあり小幅減となり、成長率
を下押し。一方、設備投資はAI関連需要の堅調さを背景にプラス転換し、成長率を押し上げ 
○ 年度後半の日本経済は、小幅なプラス成長を予想。インフレ再加速を受け消費抑制が続く一方、設
備投資が堅調に拡大。日本銀行は12月会合で政策金利を1.50％に引き上げる見込み

7～9月期の実質GDP（1次
速報値）は、ほぼ横ばい 
内閣府が本日公表した2026年7～9月期の「四半期別GDP速報（QE）」（ 1次
速報値）では、実質GDPが前期比▲0.0％（年率▲0.1％）と、ほぼ横ばいの
結果になった。4～6月期の前期比＋0.4％（年率＋1.7％）から減速し、経済
の緩やかな拡大傾向が一服した格好だ（図表）。 
需要項目別にみると、内需の柱である個人消費の減少や、輸出の小幅減少
が成長率の下押し要因になった。一方、設備投資は堅調に拡大し、成長率を
押し上げた。その他では、原油の代替調達進展を反映した輸入の増加がマイ
ナス要因になったが、前期の備蓄原油放出の反動が公的在庫投資のプラス要
因として現れ、両者の成長率に対する影響は概ね打ち消し合っている。以下、
需要項目別に7～9月期の経済動向を詳しく確認しよう。

前期比に対する寄与度（％Pt）

### Page 2

2

個人消費は2四半期連続
で減少。根強い節約志向
や、耐久財の特需剥落が
影響 
個人消費は前期比▲0.3％と、2四半期連続で減少した（4～6月期は同＋
0.0％から同▲0.3％に下方修正）。足元の消費の弱さの背景には、複合的な
要因があると考えられる。 
品目別の内訳をみると、衣料品などの半耐久財が前期比▲1.4％（4～6月
期：同＋0.4％）と、大幅に減少した。長雨などの天候不順の影響で、衣料
品の消費が弱含んだとみられる。 
食品・エネルギー・各種消耗品などの非耐久財は、前期比▲0.2％（ 4～6月
期：同▲1.9％ ）と 2四半期連続で減少した。物価高を背景とする根強い節約
志向が影響したようだ。今回のインフレ局面が本格化する前の2020年と比較
すると、必需品が多い非耐久消費財の物価は足元までの約6年半でおよそ1.3
倍の水準に上昇した計算になる。こうした累積的な価格上昇が、消費者の体
感物価の高まりを通じて節約志向を強め、購入数量抑制につながっている可
能性がある。 
また、耐久財が前期比▲3.5％（4～6月期：同＋3.6％）と大幅減に転じた
ことも、7～9月期の消費を下押しした。これは、自動車とエアコンの「特需」
剥落が原因だ。自動車は、購入時にかかる自動車税の環境性能割が3月末に
廃止されたことを受け、4月から自動車販売が過去平均に比べ上振れていた
が、8月以降は再び過去平均並みの水準に戻っている。エアコンは、2027年
4月の省エネ基準引き上げで販売価格が上昇するとの見方から駆け込み需要
が生じていたが、景気ウォッチャー調査のコメントなどをみると、8月に入
った後は一服した模様だ。9月は気温が低かったほか、長雨による客足の減
少もエアコンなど季節家電の売り上げに悪影響を及ぼしたとみられる。 
サービス消費は前期比＋0.1％（4～6月期：同▲0.1％）とプラスに転じた
が、伸びは緩慢だ。イベント関連支出などで底堅さがみられる一方、日常的
な支出には物価高による節約志向が波及し、サービスや価格帯、顧客層によ
る二極化が進みつつあるとみられる。また、8～9月には台風・長雨による旅
行・宿泊のキャンセルや外出控えの影響から、レジャー関連を中心に消費が
伸び悩んだようだ。 
設備投資は3四半期ぶり
に前期比プラス 
一方、設備投資は前期比＋1.6％と、3四半期ぶりに前期比プラスへ転じた。
4～6月期も同▲0.9％から同▲0.4％と、依然マイナスながらも上方修正され
た。 
設備投資の関連指標では、資本財の国内向け供給量を表す資本財総供給指
数（除く輸送機械）や、第3次産業活動指数におけるソフトウェア業の実質
売上高が改善している。世界的なAIブームを背景とするデータセンター等の
AI関連投資や、人手不足を受けたソフトウェア導入などの省力化投資が影響
したとみられる。 
ただし、7～9月期の変化率には、前期発生した一時的な押し下げ圧力（特

### Page 3

3

許権の海外譲渡による国内研究開発投資の減少）の反動も影響している。足
元の設備投資の伸びは実態よりもやや強い結果とみられ、多少割り引いて評
価する必要があろう。 
住宅投資は前期比マイナ
スが継続 
住宅投資は前期比▲1.1％（4～6月期：同▲0.6％ ）と 、2四半期連続で前期
比マイナスになった。中東情勢を背景とする資材不足は徐々に緩和している
とみられるが、7～9月の住宅着工件数が伸び悩んでおり、住宅価格や金利上
昇による需要低迷に加えて、地震や台風などによる着工・進捗の遅れが発生
した可能性がある。 
民間在庫は成長率へのプ
ラス寄与が縮小 
民間在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度は、±0.0％Pt（4～6月期：
＋0.3％Pt）とプラス幅が縮小した。4～6月期は石油化学製品などの供給不
足を懸念した前倒し発注が在庫を押し上げたとみられるが、7～9月期は原油
の代替調達進展に伴って供給不足が緩和したことで、そうした要因の影響が
一巡した模様だ。 
公的需要は備蓄原油放出
の反動で前期比プラス 
公的需要は前期比＋1.8％（4～6月期：同▲0.9％）と、2四半期ぶりに前期
比プラスとなった。 
最大の要因は、公的在庫変動の実質GDP成長率に対する寄与度がプラスに
転じたことだ。4～6月期は国家備蓄原油の放出により公的在庫変動の寄与度
が▲0.5％Ptと大幅マイナスになったが、7～9月期は国家原油備蓄の減少が
小幅なものにとどまり（6月末：105日分→9月末：102日分）、公的在庫の減
少ペースが緩和することで、成長率に対し＋0.5％Ptとプラス寄与した。 
一方、その他の公的需要項目では、公共投資が前期比▲1.1％（4～6月期：
同▲0.6％）と2四半期連続のマイナスになった。台風等により土木関連工事
の進捗に遅れが発生した可能性がある。また、政府消費は前期比▲0.0％（ 4
～6月期：同＋1.5％）とほぼ横ばいにとどまった。4～6月期は高校授業料・
小学校給食費の無償化開始により高い伸びになったが、7～9月期はそうした
特殊要因が剥落した。 
原油代替調達で輸入が大
幅に増加し、外需はマイ
ナス寄与に反転 
外需では、輸出が前期比▲0.2％（4～6月期：同＋1.6％）と小幅に減少し
た一方、輸入が同＋2.5％（4～6月期：同▲1.3％）と大幅に増加した。その
結果、実質GDP成長率に対する外需の寄与度は▲0.6％Pt（4～6月期：同＋
0.6％Pt）と、マイナスに反転した。 
輸入の大幅な増加は、原油の代替調達進展が主因だ。中東情勢の緊迫化に
より4～6月期は一時的に原油輸入が急減したが、7～9月期は米国産原油やホ
ルムズ海峡を通過しない中東産原油の調達が増え、9月を除いて前年並みの
輸入量が確保されている。 
こうした輸入の大幅増加は、7～9月期のGDP成長率に対し▲0.5％Ptのマイ
ナス寄与になった。もっとも、先述したように代替調達の進展に伴って国家
備蓄原油の放出が抑制され、公的在庫変動が＋0.5％Ptのプラス寄与になっ

### Page 4

4

ており、輸入増による成長率押し下げ効果は公的在庫変動によりほぼ相殺さ
れた。 
輸出は、米国関税影響で落ち込んだ2025年7～9月期以来、4四半期ぶりの
前期比マイナスになった。内訳をみると、サービス輸出が前期比▲7.1％（ 4
～6月期：同＋8.9％ ）と 急減したことが主因だ。前期の4～6月期に特許権の
海外譲渡がサービス輸出を押し上げたが、7～9月期はその反動減が現れたた
めであり（これは先述した7～9月期の設備投資の押し上げ要因でもある）、
一時的な動きと言えよう。なお、インバウンド需要（非居住者家計の国内で
の直接購入）は、前期比▲2.4％（4～6月期＋1.6％）と減少した。日中対立
の継続による中国人訪日客の少なさや、航空運賃上昇を受けた欧米訪日客の
伸び悩みが影響したとみられる。 
一方、財輸出は前期比＋2.4％（ 4～6月期：同▲0.8％）と堅調に回復した。
旺盛なAI関連需要を背景に、半導体・電子部品や半導体製造装置などの財輸
出がけん引したようだ。今後はサービス輸出の特殊要因が剥落するなか、財
輸出の拡大傾向が続き、輸出は全体として増加基調をたどると考えられる。 
2026 年度後半の実質GDP
は小幅プラスと予想。個
人消費が伸び悩む一方、
設備投資が堅調拡大 
以上のとおり、7～9月期の日本経済は前期のプラス成長から減速し、ほぼ
横ばいの結果になった。先行きについてみると、2026年度後半（2026年10～
2027年3月）の実質GDPは、均してみれば小幅なプラス成長になると予想する。
個人消費が精彩を欠く一方で、設備投資の拡大が支えになる構図が続くだろ
う。2026年度全体の実質GDP成長率は、前年度末の堅調な伸びで今年度の平
均水準が押し上げられることもあり（いわゆるプラスのゲタ）、前年比＋1％
弱とまずまずの成長率になる計算だ。 
年度後半の個人消費では、インフレ再加速による実質賃金の伸び悩みが消
費拡大の抑制要因になるだろう。原油・ナフサ価格高騰の影響が徐々に波及
し、コア消費者物価指数（生鮮食品を除く総合）は前年比＋2％台後半まで
上昇すると予想される（電気・ガス料金支援の再開と、ガソリン補助金縮小
を考慮）。また、2027年4月の消費税減税開始前には、保存食品などを中心
とした買い控えの動きが生じるとみられ、一時的に消費の抑制要因になろ
う。ただし、2026年12月～2027年2月に政府の電気・ガス料金支援が実施さ
れることで、2027年1～3月期を中心に消費が下支えされる見込みだ。 
年度後半の設備投資は堅調に拡大するだろう。日銀短観（9月調査）では、
2026年度の設備投資計画（ソフトウェア・研究開発を含む設備投資額（除く
土地投資額））が前年比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持した（6月調査：同＋8.8％ ）。
生産・営業用設備投資判断DIが2025年度後半以降に低下し、設備の不足感が
強まっていることをあわせて考えれば、設備投資は今後拡大傾向で推移して
いく見込みだ。 
また、政府の投資支援策である「大胆な投資促進税制」（特定生産性向上

### Page 5

5

設備等投資促進税制）が2026年7月末から開始されたことも、今後の設備投
資にとってプラス要因になろう。同税制における減税規模の政府見込みを参
考に試算すると、2026～2028年度に設備投資の前年比変化率を約0.1％Ptず
つ押し上げる効果があると考えられる。 
ただし、日銀が来年にかけて政策金利を一段と引き上げ、企業の新規借入
金利がさらに上昇すると予想されること、また、投資財の価格が上昇傾向を
続けると考えられることを踏まえると、中小企業を中心に設備投資を控える
企業が今後増加する可能性がある点には注意が必要だ。 
日銀の10月会合は、物価
目標到達を確認した歴史
的な日に。今後は、12月会
合で追加利上げを行う見
込み 
10月29・30日に開催された金融政策決定会合において、日本銀行は政策金
利を現行の1.25％で据え置いた。事前に報じられていたように、今回会合で
日銀は「消費者物価の基調的な上昇率は2％の「物価安定の目標」と概ね整
合的な水準」との見解を示した。2％の「物価安定の目標」を定めた2013年
1月から数えて、13年9カ月でようやく物価目標に到達した格好だ。これまで
長きにわたり実施してきたデフレ脱却のための特別な金融政策が、ついに終
わりを迎えたという意味で、歴史的な日になったと言える。 
今後は、基調的なインフレ率を2％程度の水準で「定着」させるために、物
価上振れ・下振れのリスクを都度評価して金融政策を調整していくことにな
る。当面は、中東情勢、AI関連需要、為替相場の変動（円安圧力）などを背
景に物価上振れリスクが強い状況が続くと見込まれ、日銀は金融環境への影
響を確認しながら利上げを継続するだろう。 
次の利上げ（1.25％→1.50％）は、次回12月17・18日会合と予想する。前
回利上げを行った9月会合に続いて「3カ月に1回」ペースでの利上げになり、
日銀の利上げペースが6月以前の「半年強に1回」から加速していることを印
象付けるものになろう。最終的には、2027年半ばにかけて政策金利を2.0％
まで引き上げていくとみている。 
なお、経済見通しの詳細については、みずほ総合研究所調査部「2026年・
2027年 みずほ世界経済見通し ─ 転換点は近いのか、投資主導経済の行方
を探る ─」（10月2日）をご覧いただきたい。 
（https://www.mizuhobank.co.jp/corporate/mhri/research/report/pdf/outlook261002.pdf）

2  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/
上週重要市場資訊
⚫ 美國 9 月 ISM 非製造業 PMI 從 8 月的 55.4 降至 54.9，顯示服務業活動增速放
緩，然而國內需求強勁使供應鏈承壓，並推動投入價格指數由 72.6 升至 74.0 為
四年多來新高；另一方面，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心指數由 9 月的 48.1
降至 46.3，為連續第三個月下降且創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期由 4.6%
升至 4.7%，顯示通膨可能持續維持高位至 2027 年。
⚫ 聯準會 9 月 FOMC 會議紀要顯示與會官員均支持升息，並認為年底前尚需升息
一次，但對於後續升息時點及升息理由上存在分歧；其中部分官員認為升息是防
止能源及其他價格衝擊造成更廣泛影響的預防性措施，但更為鷹派官員則認為
升息在於抑制投資和支出，以防範正在顯現的需求驅動型通膨。
⚫ 歐洲央行 9 月貨幣政策會議紀要顯示全體決策委員均同意升息，並認為當前經
濟成長風險偏向下行，通膨風險則偏向上行，因此將保留政策彈性，根據經濟數

據做為決策，不提供未來利率路徑指引。
⚫ 台灣 9 月 CPI 年增率自 8 月 2.05%上升至 2.73%，已連續 5 個月超過 2%通膨警
戒線，且創兩年半來最大漲幅，除了豪雨導致蔬果價格上漲及中秋假期的短期因
素外，油價居高不下亦使通膨壓力增加。
⚫ 中國央行發布匯率政策立場聲明，強調中國貿易發展根源於產業國際競爭力的
提升，無必要通過匯率貶值獲取貿易競爭優勢，並指出中國長期實施以市場供需
為基礎、參考一籃子貨幣進行調節管理浮動匯率制度。

### Page 3

3  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
 美元兌新台幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週台幣匯價高點在 31.699，低點在 31.940，以 31.875 作收，較前一週收盤價
貶值 4.5 分。 
上週台幣震盪走貶，週初，受到前週公布的美國非農就業數據低於預期，降低
市場對聯準會 10 月升息的預期，台股創高吸引外資匯入，推升台幣最高來到 31.699；
不過隨著美伊緊張情勢升溫且台灣連假將至，台股收低使外資轉為大幅賣超並匯出，
推低台幣回到 9 月中以來的震盪區間下緣，最低觸及 31.940，最終在央行調節之下
收在 31.875。 
＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週台幣區間震盪。就國際市場而言，美國 10 月密西根大學消費者信心
指數持續下滑，由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來最低，惟未來一年通膨預期
升至 4.7%，加上中東局勢反覆，油價高位震盪，仍支撐市場對於聯準會年內升息
的預期，預計美元指數維持在 102 附近震盪，對台幣帶來壓力；就台灣市場來看，
台灣 9 月出口創歷史新高，年增 60.9%，反映 AI 及科技需求持續強勁，出口商後
續拋匯需求可望提供台幣支撐；然而，台股逼近五萬點關卡之際，外資未能延續買
超走勢，且連假前已大幅賣超台股，若後續外資持續匯出，將限制台幣升值空間。
綜合以上，預估本週台幣區間震盪。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌台幣：31.700-32.000

4  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌人民幣匯率走勢  
Oct/12/2026 
＜上週走勢＞ 
上週人民幣區間震盪。中國十一長假期間 (10/1~10/7) 境內人民幣市場休市，而
境外人民幣維持偏強格局，主要在 6.7000 上方震盪，臨近周末，人行中間價為 6.7330，
創 3 年多來新高，加上美元指數漲勢放緩，推升境外人民幣突破 6.7000 整數關卡，
終場境外人民幣收在 6.6927，週線升值 134 點，境內人民幣收在 6.6929，較十一長
假前夕升值 121 點。

＜未來分析＞ 
    預估本週人民幣區間偏升。就國際市場來看，美國密西根大學消費者信心指數
由 9 月的 48.1 降至 46.3，創 5 月以來新低，不過對於未來一年的通膨預期升至 4.7%，
加上油價維持高檔，市場對於聯準會年內升息的預期仍存，美元指數維持高檔震盪，
將限制人民幣升值速度；就中國國內市場來看，人行中間價維持偏強設定，不過人
行表示將防範短期大幅波動與急貶風險，並無意透過人民幣貶值取得貿易競爭優勢，
有助於穩定市場對人民幣的預期；最後就技術面來看，USD/CNH 日線圖中，各天期
均線仍向下發散，空頭格局不變，下方支撐先看向 9 月底低點 6.6908。綜合以上，
預估本週人民幣區間偏升。

＜預估本週＞ 美元兌人民幣：6.6800-6.7100

5  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌日圓匯率走勢  
         Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
上週 USD/JPY 震盪走升。週初，因美元指數在 102 附近盤整，帶領 USD/JPY 延
續前一週走勢震盪走升到 158 上方；週間，因缺乏重要經濟數據，市場偏向觀望，
USD/JPY 維持在 158 附近震盪；臨近週末，USD/JPY 曾一度回測 157.50 附近，不
過在美日利差的主導下，USD/JPY 依舊保持偏多的走勢，終場收在 158.31，週線上
漲 84 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
預估本週 USD/JPY 區間震盪，主要原因如下：一、路透短觀調查顯示，受半導
體強勁訂單推動，日本 10 月大型製造業信心升至正 22，創近五年新高；但原料成
本高漲重擊非製造業，信心指數降至正 23。與此同時，此前投下升息反對票的日銀
鴿派委員佐藤綾野立場軟化，表態支持分階段升息，市場對後續正常化預期升溫，
預計將限縮 USD/JPY 的上行空間。二、日本首相高市早苗表示密切關注匯率與物價
走勢，強調日本已脫離通縮，將完全尊重央行政策獨立性，暗示不反對進一步升息。
面對通膨加劇與日圓走貶壓力，其過往堅持的貨幣寬鬆立場已出現轉向。然而目前
美日實質利差仍大，持續為 USD/JPY 提供支撐。三、就技術面而言，日線圖中，
USD/JPY 持續沿著上彎的月線上攻，然匯價持續承壓於季線下方，在有效站上季線
及突破前高壓力前，仍以區間震盪看待。綜合以上，預估 USD/JPY 本週呈現區間震
盪的可能性較高。 
＜預估本週＞ 美元兌日幣：157.10-159.50。

6  兆豐國際商業銀行 http://www.megabank.com.tw/ 
美元兌歐元匯率走勢  
             Oct/12/2026

＜上週走勢＞ 
  上週 EUR/USD 震盪走跌。週初，因法國債券風險與區域政治不確定性，
EUR/USD 下行最低來到 1.1160；週間，受中東地緣風險升溫與歐洲財政疑慮帶動避
險需求，加上美債殖利率維持高檔，美元利差優勢延續，歐元維持低檔；臨近週末，
逢低歐元有買盤支撐，帶動歐元收復部分跌幅，終場收在 1.1201，週線下跌 46 點。 
＜未來分析＞ 
  預估本週 EUR/USD 區間偏空震盪，主要原因如下：一、歐洲央行首席經濟學
家連恩表示，夏末能源成本飆升為經濟增長帶來下行風險，並可能限制央行收緊貨
幣政策的力道，與此同時，歐元區 10 月 Sentix 投資者信心指數由 5.1 降至 2.7，德
國預期指數同步滑落，反映市場對經濟復甦的信心明顯降溫，預計將令歐元持續承
壓。二、法國社會動盪與支出訴求阻礙財政減赤進程，加劇債務可持續性疑慮，並
推升法德公債利差與信用風險，令歐元承壓走弱。相較美國經濟展現韌性且聯準會
維持偏鷹立場，歐洲深陷景氣放緩與財政掣肘，歐洲央行緊縮空間受限。在歐美基
本面與政策前景持續分歧下，EUR/USD 缺乏實質支撐，後市將維持偏弱整理並下探
關鍵支撐。三、技術面而言，在日線圖中，EUR/USD 匯價雖多次向下測試 1.1165 關
鍵價位均獲得支撐，惟每次反彈均無法站上五日線，且各天期均線下彎呈現空頭排
列。綜合以上，預估 EUR/USD 本週呈現區間偏空震盪的可能性較高。

Key Data  _____________________________________

GS Forecast  __________________________________

GS Factor Profile  ______________________________

ZDT 3Q26 core business (OPI) was 10%/11% higher than GSe and 
BBG consensus, mainly thanks to the better than expected product 
mix (high margin & high growth sectors accounted for 27% of total 
revenue in 3Q26 vs. our estimate only at 20-25%). In 3Q26, ZDT GM 
was 1.8/2.6ppt higher than GSe/BBG consensus, and was 27.7% up 
4.6ppt QoQ and 5.7ppt YoY, while the annual growth was only 
driven by the better product mix/ new business contribution 
(ABF/optical module/AI PCB), even considering these new 
businesses only accounted for 27% of total revenue.  
OPEX in 3Q26 increased 24% QoQ and 56% YoY due mainly to the 
higher investment in the R&D projects for future new high-end AI 
PCB/ optical transceiver / substrates business, which was the main 
reason for the company to see a 0.7/1.4ppt higher than GSe/BBG 
consensus OPEX to sales ratio in 3Q26.  
In 4Q26, we expect ZDT revenue to go up by ~29% QoQ, mainly due 
to the solid growth from the optical transceiver, substrates, and AI 
PCB businesses, with more new AI projects to ramp in 4Q26. 
Moreover, we expect the company’s GM to further expand to ~30% 
(2.2ppt QoQ or 7.3ppt YoY) thanks to a better product mix (we 
expect high growth/high margin new business will account for 
30-35% of the company’s total revenue in 4Q26, whose average GM 
should be at least 40-45% in 4Q26).  
Investment view 
We remain positive on ZDT’s AI business outlook, and expect the 
acceleration on ZDT’s AI PCB/Substrates business revenue to further 
drive the company’s overall GM level in coming years (we expect the 
two businesses’ average GM could surpass 50% in 2027, thanks to 
the favorable supply demand condition) while we also expect the 
high GM optical transceiver (50%+ GM now) will also see at least 
120% YoY growth in 2027, suggesting a significant GM expansion 
trend for the overall company in coming quarters/years.

### Page 3

We believe ZDT will continue to be the key share gainer in the AI PCB market. For AI GPU 
projects, we are seeing ZDT share increase from <5% in 2025 to ~10-15% in 2026, 
which, we believe, will further go up to 30%+ in long term (2028 and beyond), thanks to 
the company’s proactive investment in the R&D on the latest PCB technology, as well as 
the company’s solid investment in the AI grade PCB capacity. For AI ASIC projects, the 
company’s market share is well below 10% for now, and only focus on some minor 
projects, but we do expect the company to become one of the major suppliers for AI 
ASIC projects in coming years, considering the company’s more diversified production 
sites (mainland China + Taiwan + Thailand), while most of the AI ASIC customers are 
asking for non-Greater China capacity now.  
For the optical transceiver business, we expect ZDT to see strong growth (50%+ 
2025-29E CAGR), driven mainly by the ~50% 2026-28E volume demand CAGR and the 
company’s proactive expansion in capacity in coming years (5x more capacity over the 
next two years). Moreover, ZDT currently holds an industry-leading yield of 90%+ for its 
optical module mSAP products (vs. peers only at 60-80% per our industry checks).  
For its ABF substrate business, we believe the company will continue to have the ability 
to increase ABF price, supported by full UTR and strong demand, while the industry in 
our view remains conservative on its capacity expansion plans over the next 1-2 years 
relative to our forecasts. We expect ABF substrate LTA/Spot prices to increase by 
10-15%/20%+ QoQ per quarter from 3Q26 through at least the end of 2027, with 
shortages likely extending into 2028 (see here). Overall, the business condition for ABF 
substrate is improving with price increase and we expect the business, which was loss 
making in 2025, to expand its GM to 40%+/50%+ by end of 2026/27. 
Overall, we reiterate our Buy rating on ZDT, with a new 12m TP of NT$1,070 (from 
NT$1,025).

Earnings revisions 
We revise up our 2026/27/28/29E net income estimate by 6/21/42/49% after seeing 
the solid GM expansion from these high-growth sectors, which we believe will continue 
to be the net income driver in the coming years. We believe the high growth sectors (AI 
PCB, substrates, optical module mSAP) will account for 50%+ of total revenue in 2028 
and further expand to 60%+ in 2029, with a much higher than company average GM 
(50%+ in average in 2027-28), thus we revise up our 2027/28/29E GM by 
5.4/10.7/12.4ppt vs. previous estimate.

### Page 4

Valuation 
We revise up ZDT 12m TP from NT$1,025 to NT$1,070, still based on our SOTP based 
valuation methodology of (1) 3.5x blended P/B for ABF & BT substrate (in-line with 
upcycle industry average), (2) 15x P/E for high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in 
line with industry average), and (3) 8x P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB 
(in-line with industry downcycle average valuation), and maintain our valuation period at 
4Q26-3Q27, factoring in our earnings revision.

Zhen Ding Technology (ZDT) is the global leading PCB supplier (top 1 in market share in 
sales) with 7-10% market share, and started its ABF substrate business from 2023 in 
Shenzhen, China, and has the highest ABF revenue exposure to the China market 
compared to all other ABF substrate suppliers. We believe ZDT will hold more than 40% 
ABF substrate market share in China by 2027E (up from 31% in 2024) and benefit the 
most from the strong and growing China ABF substrate market (53% 2025E-27E CAGR) 
due to the increasing in-sourcing ratio for the AI computing ASIC/GPU market in China. 
We also expect the increasing foldable smartphone penetration rate in the long term to 
drive ZDT’s FPC TAM, and account for 50% of the company’s total revenue in 2027E. We 
see further revenue upside potential from foldable phones. We are Buy rated mainly due 
to our positive view on the company’s China ABF substrate business opportunity, which 
should not only drive ZDT’s revenue growth, but also improve its OPM level in the long 
term. We see upside for the stock price considering the company’s solid growth 
opportunity, and our TP implied P/E multiple is still lower than the company’s past 10+ 
year P/E average, which we believe is fair considering the potential upside from China 
ABF substrate market and increasing foldable phone penetration rate.

high-growth AI server/switch related PCB (in line with industry average), and (3) 8x
2028E P/E for low-growth consumer electronics PCB (in line with industry downcycle
average valuation).

content upgrade, (2) delay in new substrate capacity or slower-than-expected yield rate

ramp up progress, and (3) fiercer competition in China ABF market.

## Document 150: 261111_ml_kinik

### Page 1

Kinik

Strong advanced nodes and packaging 
expansion support decent earnings

Reiterate Rating: BUY | PO: 1,050 TWD | Price: 737.00 TWD

3Q26 margin beat on favorable product mix  
Kinik’s 3Q26 sales grew 8% QoQ, mainly driven by solid diamond disk demand from N2 
and advanced packaging capacity ramp and increasing shipments of high-end 
test/reclaim wafer, more than offsetting weakness at Thailand ABU subsidiary. 3Q26 
GMs improved by 220bps QoQ to 42.2% on improving product mix across ABU and DBU 
segments, taking 3Q26 EPS to NT$3.91(vs. BofAe/Street NT$4.09/NT$4.08).  
4Q26 sales grow on continued advanced nodes expansion 
The company guided 4Q26 sales to grow slightly QoQ (BofAe + 2% QoQ), implying full-
year revenue growth of +25% YoY, backed by strength across diamond disk, test wafer, 
and reclaim wafer amid ongoing advanced nodes capacity build-out. Strong contribution 
from high-margin DBU segment, coupled with a favorable mix shift in ABU driven by 
ABF diamond wheels, should lift 4Q26 GMs further to 43.5%, in our view.  
Solid DBU demand and SBU ramp underpin '27-28  
On accelerated 3nm, 2nm and A16 capacity ramp at full utilization, alongside benign 
mature node supply/demand landscape and steady engagement with Intel on 18A/14A, 
we forecast Kinik’s diamond disk shipment to +48% CAGR over '26-28. Meanwhile, 
Kinik’s SBU sales could deliver +22% CAGR over '26-28 as 10% price hike on selected 
products take effect from early 2027 and carrier wafer demand from Micron HBM grow 
more meaningfully from 2H27. Beyond its existing business, grinding wheels for 
advanced packaging are currently under customer qualification, and would provide 
upside potential with TAM much larger than diamond disk.  
Diverse drivers and margin expansion warrant re-rating 
We keep our 2026/27/28 EPS largely unchanged at NT$14.98/26.23/36.84. Reiterate our 
Buy rating and PO of NT$1,050, still based on 32x 2H27-1H28E P/E (vs. its historical 
range 9-42x), supported by robust sales/earnings CAGR of +32%/+57% over '26-28.

Company Sector 
Semiconductor Capital Equipment

Company Description 
Kinik was established in 1953 as a conventional grinding 
wheels supplier. Since 1990-2000s, the company has 
transformed itself into a semiconductor consumables 
supplier including CMP pad conditioners (diamond disks) 
and reclaim/test wafers.

Investment Rationale 
We have a Buy rating on Kinik. We acknowledge its 
technology for CMP diamond disk is solid to support its 
gain in TSMC advanced nodes and Intel along with growing 
reclaimed and testing wafer demand. We expect to see 
margins expansion from mix upgrade in coming years.

### Page 4

Price objective basis & risk 
Kinik (KIKCF) 
Our Price Objective of NT$1,050 is based on 32x 2H27-1H28E P/E, which is at upper 
half of the company's historical trading range of 9-42x. In our view, the valuation 
multiple is underpinned by 
1) around +57% earnings CAGR in 2026-28E supported by +32% sales CAGR growth and 
GMs/OpMs expansion, 2) ROE expansion to around 30% in 2025-28E, 4) net cash of 
around NT$30/share, and 5) free cash flow generation of around NT$50/share per 
annum across 2025-27E.

Downside risks to our estimates and expectations are 
1) weaker market demand, especially in the cloud AI investment moderation, leading to 
lower utilization across its major foundry and memory customers and undermining the 
company's revenue growth and operating leverage 
2) slower manufacturing technology migration by its foundry and memory customers, 
dragging the product upgrade cycle for Kinik and potentially spurring more competition 
from its peers. 
3) failure to execute or delay of technology roadmap, potentially leading to an order shift 
to its competitors.

ASMedia Technology Inc  | Asia Pacific
Inventory correction extends
into 1Q27
Inventory adjustment extends into 1Q27. Management sees improving
motherboard sell-through, but expects customer destocking to constrain shipments
in 4Q26–1Q27. AM4 exceeded 70% of chipset shipments in 3Q and should remain
dominant in 4Q, weighing on ASPs. Management indicated GM of around 50% in
4Q26–1Q27 as foundry and backend costs rise; alternative packaging/testing sources

should begin helping from 2Q27. October's stronger proprietary mix provided some
relief, but 2027 revenue guidance remains unfinalized, with 20% growth now
considered aggressive.
Recovery likely depends on execution from 2H27. ASMedia signed a new SOW
with the customer and currently sees itself as the sole supplier. Management hopes
shipments will start in 2Q27, with meaningful platform volume from 3Q27; ASPs
would be at least 50–60% above AM4. PCIe switches contributed 6–8% of revenue

after Gen4 yield and thermal issues delayed design-ins, versus a double-digit mix
target for 2027. USB4 is expected to more than double from 2026's depressed base,
although robotics remains small. New products can support recovery, but higher
development spending limits near-term operating leverage.

Valuation Methodology and Risks
ASMedia Technology Inc (5269.TW)
Base case, residual income model. Key assumptions: cost of equity of 9.8% (beta 1.3, risk-free
rate 2% and risk premium 6%), medium-term growth rate of 8.0%, payout ratio of 75% and
terminal growth rate of 5.2%.
Risks to Upside
n AMD gains market share thanks to better-than-expected performance.
n In-house designed products grow faster than expected.
n US-China tensions improve and US tariffs alleviate.

Risks to Downside
n AMD cannot get substrate supply, causing earnings downside for ASMedia.
n Intel adopts TSMC's technologies more aggressively to compete with AMD.
n Spec migration on USB 4 is slower.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

below.

Cautious (C): The analyst views the performance of his or her industry coverage universe over the next 12-18 months with caution vs. the relevant broad market benchmark, as indicated below.

기아 (000270) 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결

▶Analyst 김성래 sr.kim@hanwha.com 3772-7751

Buy (유지) 
목표주가(하향): 260,000원

배당수익률 5.8  6.0  6.2  6.7  
ROE 19.1  12.9  12.7  12.9

주가 추이

견조한 판매 확대로 매출 견조, 이익은 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 33.6조원(+17.3% 이하 YoY)과 2.39조
원(+63.6%) 기록 전망. 3Q 매출액은 친환경차 중심 신차 확대 통해 두 자릿수 
성장세를 기록. 3Q 판매량은 도매 기준 84.3만대(+7.6% 이하 YoY)로 전년 동
기 대비 성장 폭을 확대했고, 특히 친환경차 판매량 29.8만대(+45.9%)를 기록
하며 이번 분기 매출 성장에 절대적으로 기여.  
다만, 3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입 영향과 ‘경제형EV 믹스’가 더
욱 확대(13.2% → 14.5%)되면서 단기 수익성에 부정적 작용하며 시장 기대치 
2.5조원을 다소 하회할 전망. 그러나, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지
면서 전년 동기 수익성 악화에 크게 영향을 미쳤던 판매보증비 외화환산손익 
영향이 개선(1.6%p 수준) 기대되며, 관세 부담 또한 북미 HEV 확대로 전년 동
기 대비 약 4,500억원(2.0%p) 감소하면서 부정적 수익성 영향 만회 전망.  
유럽은 점유율 확대, 북미는 수익성 개선으로 
올해 북미 HEV와 유럽 BEV 중심 신차 출시 및 물량 확대가 본격화되면
서, ’27년에는 이익 성장 기여도가 확대될 것으로 전망. 미국 조지아 텔루라이
드 HEV 신규 생산(2.8만대) 및 HMGMA 스포티지HEV신규 생산(1.0만
대) 등 수요가 높은 북미 현지에서 HEV 물량 확대 중으로 4Q에도 텔루라이
드 HEV와 스포티지 HEV의 현지 생산/판매 확대를 비롯하여 셀토스 HEV 
출시로 ’27년 북미 중심 HEV 신차 물량 증가 및 믹스 개선 기대. 유럽은 강한 
BEV 수요가 지속되면서 중국 업체들의 공세 강화 중이나 동사의 물량 확대 또
한 견조. 3Q EV3 2.9만대, EV5 2.2만대 등 유럽 BEV 수요 대응 전략 주효. ’27
년에는 K4/셀토스 및 쏘렌토 등 HEV 신차 중심의 친환경차 PT 믹스 다양화
로 유럽 내 물량 확대 흐름 지속 전망 
물량 확대는 증익으로 귀결, 기말 배당 매력도 부각 
’27년 동사 영업이익 추정치는 11.1조원(+13.5%)으로 증익 지속 전망. 최근 
환율 추세 고려 시 내년 수익성에 부정적 영향 확대 가능성 존재하나, 올해 확
인된 신차 중심 물량 확대를 통해 부정적 환율 영향을 상쇄할 것으로 기대. 
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-2.9%) 및 상반기 적용한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)로 목표주가를 260,000원으로 하향. 그러나, RA 설립에서의 동사 지분 
참여 및 기말 배당(’26년 DPS 7,000원 추정)에 따른 상대적 배당 수익 매력
도 부각 등은 주가 모멘텀에 있어 기대 가능한 요소.

반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

기아 (000270) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

[ 당사 조사분석자료의 투자등급 부여 비중 ] (기준일: 2026 년 9 월 30 일) 
투자등급 매수 중립 매도 합계 
금융투자상품의 비중 96.4% 3.6% 0.0% 100.0%

前，地緣政治風險不減，添股市賣壓。中國
長鑫存儲在新型晶片研發取得進展，衝擊半
導體類股，美光與三星、海力士記憶體廠即
使仍供不應求，亦遭市場拋售，南韓股指週

望，透露部分客戶已洽詢2029年至2030年設
備訂單，顯示晶片擴產需求仍強。台積電3Q

量與海外廠產能利用率造成稀釋，但今年資
本支出財測維持600到640億美元不變，反映

資本支出將較過去三年進一步顯著提高。

► 美國9 月CPI 、PPI 皆上升， 且 超 過 市 場 預
期，9月PPI月增率為1.3%，創2022年來最

達6.3%，為2022年12月來最大漲幅，顯示
通膨壓力正從能源擴大至其他領域。通膨僵
固性使市場預期高利率將維持更久時間，美

險資金轉入，公債止跌回升。體質較弱的企
業債出現警訊，CoreWeave債券殖利率大幅
攀升， 2032 年 到 期 債 券 殖 利 率 一 度 升 至

力，除違約風險上升，過高成本侵蝕企業獲
利、股債皆挫，顯示目前即使是AI、科技信
用債表現也開始出現分化。

膨預期。虛擬資產比特幣、以太幣隨風險性
資產同步下挫。

► 美國9月CPI年增率上升至3.7%，核心CPI年
增率由2.4%升至2.6%，核心月增率0.3%，
高於預期值0.2%。能源是通膨反彈的主因，
貢獻整體CPI月增幅超過一半；能源衝擊雖
為主導，但消費韌性使企業保有成本轉嫁能
力，住房與部分服務價格維持黏性，需求面
通膨略有提高，加上企業資本支出提升經濟
與企業獲利韌性，使總需求不易快速降溫。
► 凱投預期Fed 12月將升息1碼，明年3月再升
1碼，政策利率可能在4%以上維持更久。受
到油價高盤、通膨僵固，與財政惡化、政府
發債籌資逢企業債搶錢等影響，美歐日主要
國家公 債利 率近 期 來到高 位 。其中， 法國
2027年財政預算欲刪減支出(包括養老金、公
部門工資等)引發民眾反彈，衝擊主權信用評
等，法國CDS與法德10年期公債利差擴大、
歐元走弱，美元轉強，商品、黃金承壓。法
國政治不確定性讓歐股修正、法股最疲，成
熟國家公債殖利率升高，增加企業借貸成本
與股市評價壓力，續為金融市場擾動因子。

資料來源：Bloomberg，Factset，Goldman Sachs，凱基證券整理，註：右上圖之大型雲端服務供應商(CSP)企業為Amazon、Alphabet、Microsoft、Oracle
► 美國第三季財報季展開，市場將觀察企業在
高利率環境下的獲利表現。根據FactSet數據
顯示，標普500指數第三季獲利成長預估達
29.5%，顯示企業基本面仍具支撐，其中科
技 產業 受 惠AI需求帶動， 獲 利 成 長預 估達
65%，持續扮演企業獲利的重要成長動能。
► 聚焦科技類股，AI資本支出仍為本輪企業獲
利成長的主要驅動力。高盛指出，第三季AI

基礎建設相關企業將貢獻標普500獲利增長
的54%。此外，大型CSP之雲端業務營收有
望加速成長，反映AI運算及雲端服務需求持
續提升，有助支撐後續資本支出擴張動能。
► 近期市場波動仍受高利率環境影響，壓抑投
資人風險偏好與估值擴張空間，顯示市場目
前壓力更多來自評價面而非基本面。隨財報
數據陸續公布，若企業持續繳出優於預期的
獲利表現，並維持正向財測，市場有望逐步
回歸基本面評價，使企業獲利成長逐步支撐
股市表現。
美企財報獲利穩健，對股市中長期有撐

評價面疑慮下，股價難以進一步擴張

投資焦點
► 醫療保健、醫療服務與生物技術等行業，在
景氣不好時，市場預期民眾對醫療保健產業
的需求 不易 下降 ， 成為傳 統防禦 型類 股代
表。目前金融環境利率偏高，11月美國總統
大選國會恐變天，股市波動風險上升，觀察
過去當VIX恐慌指數上揚時，醫療保健產業
相較於科技股更穩健，具有更好抗震能力。
► 醫療與生技類股近一年漲幅明顯，主因1)生
技股過去一段時間受到高利率、融資成本及
臨床試驗風險壓抑，部分公司估值偏低。當
臨床數據改善、或導入AI篩選資料與藥物，
投資人重新評價未來現金流與價值。2)大型
藥廠面臨既有藥品專利到期壓力，透過收購
取得新藥與研發管線，提高有潛力的生技公
司併購價值，帶動整體產業估值回升。3)肥
胖、癌症與慢性病用藥創造長期市場需求，
創新藥物(如瘦瘦針)讓部分醫療公司成長不
再只依賴人口老化，高盛今年7月將全球減肥
藥市場2030年銷售額預估上調15%至1,140
億美元，看好此類藥品的長期商業潛力。
醫療生技近年表現佳，估今年3Q醫院客流量年增4%VIX恐慌指數上升時，健護醫療表現相較穩健

市場波動上升、醫療保健表現佳，獲利穩定成長

### Page 8

內部參考文件請勿外流
► 美國醫療費用深受法令影響，由於商業雇主
贊助保險(ESI)和平價醫療法案(ACA)的自付
額增加，以及最高自付費用要求，較去年同
期提高，使今年第三季美國整體住院人數、
手術量和醫院客流量均呈現改善。高盛預估
今年第三季醫院客流量年增4%，加上季節性
因素，下半年醫療保健利用率將較上半年成
長，整體第四季預估獲利穩健，甚至可延至
明年，反應在近期XLV ETF股價創新高。
► 展望2027年，各醫療保險計畫普遍優先考慮
透過提高會員保費，和要求費用分攤(尤其是
提高會員自付費用上限)來擴大利潤空間，並
持續優化覆蓋範圍和產品組合，預估商業雇
主提供的醫療保險(ESI)保費趨勢，將在明年
加速成長至過去20 年來的最高水準。 以
Refinitiv預估明年美國各產業獲利成長來看，
醫療保健和生技相較於今年獲利明顯增加，
反而今年獲利極佳的能源和通訊受高基期影
響，獲利增速下滑。投資人在整體金融環境
波動放大時期，宜以醫療生技股穩中求利。
投資焦點
近3個月漲幅和預估獲利呈正成長，醫療生技股佳

股市
 美股第三季獲利年增率預估為雙位數成長，市場靜待財報結果與展望，和對AI資本支出回收影響。
考量地緣衝突、殖利率偏高、評價面壓力與鄰近美國期中選舉，短期金融市場紛擾不止，可增加防
禦股增強耐震度。長期AI、科技發展趨勢不變，逢股市拉回時可分批布局，分散配置於不同國家與
產業，降低市場整體波動，亦可以槓鈴策略(防禦+成長)穩中求利。
 歐、日股可作為分散配置重點，全球利率走揚有利銀行股。此外，地緣衝突頻傳，歐洲國防軍工仍
有投資題材。
策略風格：可增加防禦股配置，中長期看
好AI題材如科技及半導體，非AI部分關注
醫療保健生技產業
區域：日本銀行股、歐洲金融、歐洲國防
債市
 全球公債殖利率攀升，預估今年底前主要國家仍有升息空間，殖利率偏高環境使短中期債券提供吸
引力，逢利率走高、鎖利領息。高信評債體質較佳，具穩定現金流與還款能力，聚焦風險調整後利
差具吸引力者，包含金融、科技、通訊及公用事業等產業。
債種：中短天期高信評債鎖利，產業如金
融、科技、通訊及公用事業等

匯市
 歐洲政治風險上升、歐元短期弱盤，美元高檔有撐。
 日本銀行再度升息時間不定，日圓區間震盪。

人買盤依舊，提供黃金中長期穩定支撐。

個別客戶因年齡、投資能力、金融商品交易經驗及風險承受度不同，適合之服務及/或金融商品亦有不同，應進一步與專屬理財業務人員進行評估。投資人應徹底瞭解相關服務及金融商品之風險，並審慎評估個人財力、資產狀況、
理財需求及風險承受能力，獨立判斷並自行決定是否投資及承擔有關風險。投資任何商品皆具有風險，商品以往之績效不保證未來之投資收益，本公司不負責商品之盈虧，也不保證最低之收益。
本公司保留對本文件及其內容之著作權及其他相關權利，任何人未經本公司同意，不得翻印或作其他任何用途使用(包括但不限於傳送或轉發予其他人士等)。如有違反情事，本公司除不負任何責任外，亦將對未經同意之使用與散布
等行為究責。
基金警語
投資一定有風險，基金投資有賺有賠，申購前應詳閱公開說明書
【信託產品投資須知及風險預告：（請投資人審慎評估下列各項條文內容）】
1. 基金（包括具有定期配息之基金）投資具投資風險，此一風險可能使信託本金發生全部虧損。
2. 基金委託人須自負盈虧，本公司受託投資不保本不保息，除盡善良管理人注意義務外，投資所可能產生的本金虧損、匯率損失、或基金解散、清算、移轉、合併等風險，均由委託人承擔。

3. 本公司受託投資之基金經金管會核准或同意生效，惟不表示絕無風險。基金經理公司以往之經理績效不保證基金之最低投資收益；基金經理公司除盡善良管理人之注意義務外，不負責基金之盈虧，亦不保證最低之收益，委託人
申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書。
4. 投資前應詳閱風險預告及各該基金公開說明書，確實作好財務規劃與風險評估。本公司受託投資之基金已備有公開說明書(或其中譯本)或投資人須知【前述文件併揭露有關基金應負擔之費用(境外基金含分銷費用)】，委託人可
至本公司營業處所索取或至公開資訊觀測站、基金資訊觀測站或本公司網站下載。
5. 為保護既有投資人之權益，部份境外基金設有價格稀釋調整機制及公平市價規定，投資人申購前應詳閱基金公開說明書及投資人須知。
6. 委託人瞭解並同意受託人辦理特定金錢信託業務(即委託人委託投資基金)交易時，自交易相對人取得之報酬、費用、折讓等各項利益，得作為受託人收取之信託報酬；另委託人在持有基金期間，受託人仍持續收受經理費分成報
酬，委託人可至受託人(即本公司)網站 (https://www.kgi.com.tw/zh-tw/)查詢基金之通路報酬。其基金經理費率及其分成費率與其他通路報酬最新變動情形，亦可至該網站查詢。

7. 後收型基金在贖回時，基金公司將依持有期間長短收取不同比率之遞延申購手續費，該費用將自贖回總額中扣除；另後收型基金若有收取分銷費，則申購手續費雖可遞延收取，惟每年仍需支付0%~1.06%的分銷費（按基金日平
均淨資產價值計算），可能造成實際負擔費用增加（分銷費依各基金公司而有不同，請參考本公司後收型基金投資費用暨特約事項及各基金之投資人須知），另該費用已反映於每日基金淨值中，委託人無需額外支付。
8. 委託人之交易如經基金公司認定為短線交易者，基金公司或本公司可拒絕受理申請轉換或申購要求，並可請求支付一定比例之贖回費用或其他相關費用，費用標準均依各該基金公司之規定。
9. 委託人因不同時間進場，將有不同之投資績效，過去之績效亦不代表未來績效之保證。基金配息率不代表基金報酬率，且過去配息率不代表未來配息率；基金淨值可能因市場因素而上下波動，投資人於獲配息時，宜一併注意基
金淨值之變動。又投資新興市場可能比投資已開發國家有較大的價格波動及流動性較低的風險，委託人應慎選投資標的。
10. 部分基金可能持有衍生性商品部位，該部位可能超過基金淨資產價值之100%，並可能因而造成基金淨值高度波動及衍生其他風險。

11. 基金的配息可能由基金的收益或本金中支付。任何涉及由本金支出的部分，可能導致原始投資金額減損；且部分基金配息前未先扣除應負擔之費用。由本金支付配息之相關資料，投資人可至證券投資信託事業或總代理人之公司
網站查詢。
12. 非投資等級債券基金（本類基金主要係投資於非投資等級之高風險債券且配息來源可能為本金）（主要特色係投資前揭基金之組合基金亦同），適合能承受較高風險之『非保守型』委託人且不宜占投資組合過高的比重。由於非
投資等級債券之信用評等未達投資等級或未經信用評等，且對利率變動的敏感度甚高，可能會因利率上升、市場流動性下降，或債券發行機構違約不支付本金、利息或破產而蒙受虧損，不適合無法承擔相關風險之委託人；又如
係本國投信機構募集者，則可能投資於符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，雖其投資總金額不得超過基金淨資產價值之百分之三十，然該債券具有債券發行人違約之信用風險、利率風險、流動性風險、交易對手風險
及因財務訊息揭露不完整或因價格不透明導致波動性較大之風險，投資人應審慎評估；另證券投資信託事業募集之債券型基金、平衡型基金、不動產證券化基金、以追蹤、模擬或複製標的指數表現之指數股票型基金（ETF）及

指數型基金或募集之基金投資新興市場國家債券達基金淨資產價值之百分之六十以上者，或有可能投資符合美國Rule 144A規定具有私募性質之債券，亦應留意相關風險。
13. 投資於中國之基金應遵守中華民國相關法令之規定：境外基金(不含經金管會核准向證交所申請上市或交易之境外ETF)如有投資大陸地區之有價證券者，以掛牌上市有價證券及銀行間債券市場為限，且投資前述有價證券總金額
不得超過該境外基金淨資產價值之百分之二十(境外基金機構依鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫向金管會申請並經認可者得適用鼓勵境外基金深耕計畫所列放寬前揭投資總金額上限至百分之四十之優惠措施)；證券投資信託基金雖可投資
大陸地區之有價證券，惟仍應符合信託契約、公開說明書、證券投資信託基金管理辦法等相關規定及受到基金公司QFII額度之限制。又投資人須留意中國市場之政府政策、法令、會計稅務制度、經濟與市場等變動所可能產生之
投資風險。
14. 各基金於公開（含簡式）說明書或投資人須知揭露之風險報酬等級，係依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」而訂定，本公司亦依據投信投顧公會「基金風險報酬等級分類標準」訂定基金產品風險等級。

富管理客服專線或透過財富管理客服信箱服務，查詢申訴案件處理進度。

## Document 398: September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-17)

Source PDF: all report pdf/September CPI_ Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas (2026-11-17).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 18
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #australia #rba #economicbulletinsaustralia #inflation
September CPI: Inflation accelerating in the run up to Christmas
By Justin Smirk & Neha Sharma
16:08 October 28 2026 Share
September Monthly CPI: Headline 0.8%mth/4.3%yr, Trimmed Mean 0.3.%mth/3.6%yr.
Market services ex volatile 1.6%mth/4.0%yr. September Quarter CPI: Headline
1.3%qtr/3.9%yr; Trimmed Mean 1.0%qtr/3.7%yr.
The CPI rose 0.8% in the September month, stronger than Westpac’s 0.6% estimate and the

market’s expectation of 0.7%. The annual pace picked up from 4.0%yr to 4.3%yr, The seasonally
adjusted CPI rose 0.6% in September highlighting some positive seasonality in the month.

### Page 2

Key factors driving the headline monthly result were: automotive fuels (9.4%mth); domestic holiday
travel & accommodation (8.0%mth); international holiday travel & accommodation (7.2%mth); and
property rates & charges (5.5%mth). There were modest offsets from: women’s garments (–
3.1%mth); household textiles (–6.6%mth); and accessories (–4.1%mth).
The Trimmed Mean (TM) gained 0.3% in September, spot on both Westpac and market expectations
with the annual pace lifting from 3.5%yr (revised from 3.6%yr) to 3.6%yr. The six-month annualised

pace is now 4.0%yr the fastest in the short history of this series.
Headline CPI lifted 1.3% in the September quarter compared to an expectation of 1.3% by Westpac
and 1.2% by the market. This saw the annual pace hold at 3.9%yr.
The RBA’s key underlying inflation measure, the quarterly TM, lifted 1.0% vs an expectation of
0.9% by Westpac and market estimate of 1.0%. The annual pace lifted from 3.6%yr to 3.7%yr
with the six-month annualised pace accelerating to 3.7%yr from 3.4%yr.

Overall, the estimates come in broadly as expected so we see minimal risk to our current December
quarter forecast of 1.3%qtr for the headline CPI. Softer housing inflation does present a very modest
downside risk to our 0.9%qtr for the TM but there are offsetting gains elsewhere.
The September CPI update revealed underlying inflationary pressures continuing even while a
moderation in housing inflation (dwellings in particular) is unfolding slightly faster than we had

anticipated. As such, the near-term risk for inflation remains to the upside. The RBA will remain alert
to these near-term inflationary risks and as so, we hold to the view that the RBA will raise rates again
at the November meeting.

### Page 3

Month of September CPI
The monthly CPI rose 0.8%mth in September, above both our expectation of a 0.6%mth increase and
the market forecast of 0.7%mth. This lifted the annual pace by 0.3ppts to 4.3%yr. On a seasonally
adjusted basis, the CPI increased 0.6%mth.
The key contributors to the monthly increase were: automotive fuel (9.4%mth, +0.3ppts); and holiday
travel & accommodation (7.6%mth, +0.5ppts), alongside a smaller contribution from the annual reset in

property rates (5.5%mth, +0.1ppt). There was no single major offset. Instead, several components
across household furnishings and clothing & footwear recorded modest declines, together subtracting a
little over 0.1ppt from the monthly result.
The monthly trimmed mean (TM) rose 0.3%mth, in line with both our and the market’s expectations.
The annual pace edged up to 3.6%yr, although the August outcome was revised down to 3.5%yr.
Underlying inflation has remained broadly stuck in the 3.5–3.6%yr range since May. On a 3m/3m basis,

the monthly TM was 1.1%.

### Page 4

Market services excluding volatile items and holiday travel were flat in the month, with annual inflation
easing to 3.8%yr, its softest pace since November 2025. On a seasonally adjusted basis, monthly
inflation moderated across most components in this measure, with telecommunications services, rents
and other financial services the key exceptions. However, all but two components are still recording
annual price growth above 3%, suggesting that disinflation across market services remains slow and

relatively narrow. The persistence of these underlying price pressures is likely to remain a concern for
the RBA.

### Page 5

Housing inflation was notably softer in the month, rising 0.3%mth to be 5.2% higher over the year.
Rents increased 0.2%mth, slightly below our expectation for a 0.3%mth rise. The biannual indexation
of Commonwealth Rent Assistance occurred late in the month, weighing on the September outcome,
with the remainder of the impact expected to flow through in October. The ABS noted that rents would
have risen 0.3%mth in the absence of the measure, while on a seasonally adjusted basis rents

increased 0.4%mth.
New dwelling inflation was flat in the month, the softest reading since May 2025. Monthly inflation has
now slowed in each successive month since May 2026, suggesting homebuilders’ pricing power is
becoming increasingly constrained. The ABS noted that some project home builders raised base prices
this month only to be offset by some builders increasing discounts and promotional offers in response
to slowing demand. The annual pace eased to 4.7%yr. Dwelling repair costs were also a downside

surprise, falling 0.1%mth, their first decline since February 2025, although prices remain 3.4% higher
over the year. Some of this softness was offset by a firmer-than-expected increase in property rates
and charges, which rose 5.6%mth.
Recently, we have also focused on audio, visual and computing given the potential for spillovers from
strong global demand for semiconductors and other computing resources associated with the AI build
out. Prices for both equipment and services came in weaker than August, with equipment prices falling

1.1%mth, while services rose 0.3%mth. Despite this, annual price growth remains relatively high for
these categories, running at 4.6% and 4.9% respectively. Some of this pressure might also be showing

### Page 6

through in gaming, toys and hobbies, which is running at 5.2%mth/15.5%yr.
Overall, the breadth of inflation moderated for a second consecutive month. The share of the basket
running above 3%yr fell from 40% to 35%, meanwhile the share below 2%yr lifted from 27% to 32%.
Our replication of the San Francisco Fed’s inflation shock momentum served as a good guide to the
quarterly TM. With the full monthly reads in for the September quarter in, this measure does signal
some persistence/upside pressures for the quarterly TM going into the coming quarters. It is worth
cautioning that this measure will be impacted as the ABS refines its seasonal adjustment process for
the monthly indexes.

Compared to our expectations, the key surprises were:
Upside surprises
Downside surprises
Fruit & vegetables was the key upside surprise, 0.6%mth against our expectations for 3.7%mth fall.
Holiday travel & accommodation also surprised to the upside, following a run of downside surprises
since early this year. It rose 7.6%mth against our expectations for a 4.4%mth lift. 
Automotive fuel rose 9.4%mth against expectations of an 8.3%mth lift.

### Page 7

September Quarter CPI
Clothing & footwear was the key downside surprise with prices falling 2.7%mth against our
expectations for a 0.2%mth fall. The misses were broad-based: garments (–2.3%mth vs –2.0%mth);
footwear (–2.5%mth vs 1.8%mth); and accessories & clothing services (–4.1%mth vs 2.8%mth).
Household textiles declined 6.6%mth against our expectations for a 3.2%mth fall. Furniture &
furnishings rose by less-than-expected 0.4%mth vs 1.6%mth. Household appliances, utensils and

tools also came in softer, –3.2%mth vs –2.4%mth.
Dairy prices fell 1.2%mth against our expectations for a 0.1%mth lift.

### Page 8

The September quarter CPI printed spot on our expectation of 1.3%. The key factor driving this
increase was the slightly stronger surge in auto fuel (7.8%, +0.27ppt) and holiday travel &
accommodation (2.7%qtr, +0.16ppt) being offset by a slightly softer than expected increase in housing
(1.1%qtr, +0.24ppt) with rents and dwellings both increasing 1.0% in the quarter and utilities lifting
1.2%. Food was also slightly stronger than expected (0.7%qtr, +0.12ppt) and while not significant to the
overall result it is interesting to track the ongoing rise in motor vehicle prices (0.3%qtr) despite a very
competitive market with Chinese manufacturers rapidly gaining market share over legacy brands.

It is worth noting that food was stronger than we expected (0.7% vs 0.5%) with a robust 1.7% increase
in restaurant meals and a solid 1.2% increase in take away & fast foods outweighing the –0.4% decline
in fresh fruit & vegetables and meat & seafoods. Overall, market services ex volatile prices increased a
very solid 1.2%qtr but with the annual pace easing to 3.2%yr from 3.36%yr.
Outside of housing the other main downside surprise in the quarter was the –0.6% fall in clothing &
footwear (+0.3% expected) with prices falling across just about all categories of garments, footwear
and accessories.
September Quarter Trimmed Mean

### Page 9

Recall that the TM is calculated from seasonally adjust consumption expenditure classes so any
numbers quoted here may not be the same as the original estimates quoted from the CPI. The TM gain
of 1.0% in the quarter was slightly stronger than we had expected even though dwellings (which have a
weight of almost 11% in the TM) was a touch softer at 1.0% compared to the 1.1% we had pencilled in.
Rents (1.0%, 10% weight) also made a solid contribution as did restaurant meals (1.7%qr, 6% weight),

take away & fast food (1.2%, 4% weight), other financial service (1.0%, 6% weight). Domestic travel
was in the TM (1.0%, 4% weight) as was medical & hospital service (1.0%, weight 5%). The range of
the TM was from 0.0% (motor vehicles) to 1.7% (beer).
What does this mean for our December quarter forecast and into 2027?
We are working though the monthly results to see what changes they may imply for individual
component inflationary momentum. Our preliminary analysis suggests that the softer trend in dwellings

is not enough, on its own, to change our Q4 CPI forecast of 1.3%. It does, however, suggest that if all
else held equal (which is not the case as there is likely to be some offsetting stronger gains elsewhere)
the moderation in dwelling inflation presents a very modest downside risk our Q4 TM estimate of 0.9%.
We are still working the numbers and will publish an updated inflation profile as soon as we can. In
addition, the overall momentum in underlying inflationary pressures suggest the downside risk to the

TM from dwellings is more likely to appear in 2027 than in the last quarter of 2026.

Transitioning to mid-cycle 
• We now favour a more balanced stance between stocks and bonds. Near-
term event risks and high earnings expectations prompt us to trim equities to a 
Core holding. The US and Asia ex-Japan (AxJ) remain our preferred regions. 
Within AxJ, we upgrade India to Overweight. 
• Higher bond yields are improving the outlook for future returns and offering 
increasingly attractive income opportunities. We are Overweight Emerging 
Market (EM) USD bonds and favour corporate over government bonds, but limit 
duration to 3-7 years. 
• We remain Overweight gold. Prices are likely to grind higher as near-term 
headwinds from higher bond yields are offset by EM central bank demand. 
Great expectations 
Individual risk events, high earnings expectations and a higher cost of capital 
point to a more moderate return environment ahead. In the very near term, 
financial markets must navigate key event risks, such as (i) the US mid-term elections, 
which have historically been associated with elevated market volatility and (ii) renewed 
geopolitical tensions, with several reports suggesting the US is considering renewed 
strikes on Iran. These concerns have pushed the Brent oil price well above USD 
110/bbl despite significant ongoing crude oil flows through the Strait of Hormuz.  
Higher bond yields combined with high earnings expectations raise the risk of 
an expectations reset. The US Q3 earnings season is attracting significant attention 
given high earnings expectations, particularly for the AI sector. However, higher US 
bond yields may start to bite as debt-financed capex takes on a greater share of total 
capex over the coming year. This, combined with already-high expectations, raises the 
risk of disappointment and a near-term repricing of expected earnings growth pace.  
Despite the current challenges, healthy growth continues to underpin the 
economic backdrop. The US economic outlook remains well supported, and the 
absolute level of year-ahead earnings expectations is high. We do not expect Fed rate 
hikes to result in overly restrictive policy. Our caution, thus, stems from the risk of an 
expectations repricing rather than an outright economic slowdown.

A more balanced stock-bond mix 
We trim global equities to a Core holding, leaving us 
Neutral on both equities and bonds and Overweight gold. 
The concerns laid out earlier point to near-term volatility risks 
and a somewhat lower long-term growth trajectory for 
equities. Our quant model also reflects this cautious outlook, 
having downgraded equities to a level usually associated with 
flat-to-modestly-negative returns. 
From a relative perspective, though, the higher yields on 
offer from bonds also raise the bar by presenting an 
increasingly compelling return outlook after several 
years of very low yields. We believe this argues for portfolios 
that are increasingly balanced between equities and bonds. 
Ways to diversify equity exposure 
We continue to favour global equity market 
diversification, with a preference for US and AxJ equities. 
We remain Overweight US and AxJ equities, expecting them 
to outperform other major regions. Earnings growth remains 
the main driver of this view – while growth rates could 
decelerate somewhat, we still expect them to remain higher 
than those of other major regions.  
Japan and Euro area equities remain attractive as 
regional diversifiers. Our positive view on both markets’ 
financial sectors supports our Core holding view on them.   
Within Asia, we are Overweight Taiwan and India. Indian 
equities have lagged the region this year, in part due to limited 
listed AI-related exposure. However, we believe this is 
increasingly likely to become an advantage as investors seek 
greater diversification away from excessive concentration in 
the AI theme. This comes at a time when the economic and 
earnings growth outlook for Indian equities is starting to 
improve. We retain a more Neutral view on Chinese equities 
amid a still-challenging growth outlook. 
We also favour greater sector diversification. In the US, 
we favour diversifying across our Overweight sectors of 
technology, financials, materials and healthcare. We also 
favour financials in Japan and the Euro area.  
Changes to our equity opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more defensive stance. In recent weeks, we have closed our 
global semiconductors, MSCI Taiwan, US communication 
services and global power & electrification ideas and have 
opened new ideas on US healthcare and MSCI Switzerland. 
Riding the yield train 
We believe bond markets offer an attractive opportunity 
to lock in yields. The 10-year US government bond yield is 
now in a range last witnessed in 2007. While the process of 
rising yields results in lower bond prices – as painfully 
experienced in recent months – the yield level itself starts to 
significantly improve the outlook for future returns. 
Favour corporate over government bonds, but check 
your duration. Still-high credit quality means that we continue 
to favour taking on credit risk to earn a higher yield (EM USD 
bonds are preferred, but we also see investment-grade (IG) 
and high-yield (HY) bonds as Core holdings). However, 
limiting duration to 3-7 years remains key to avoiding 
excessive exposure to any inflation surprises. 
Changes to our bond opportunistic ideas also reflect a 
more balanced stance. We close our EU bank contingent 
convertible bonds (CoCos2 – please refer page 29) and US 
utilities corporate hybrids ideas. We also close our 
opportunistic idea on US 10-year government bonds following 
the recent pullback in yields, locking in gains. 
Alternatives and gold to remain relevant 
Alternatives are an effective tool for portfolio risk 
management. Hedge funds and private markets provide a 
differentiated return stream, while rotating into alternatives 
helps reduce equity beta exposure. We examine downside 
capture and how beta is reduced via hedge fund sub-
strategies and private markets in our asset allocation section.  
Gold remains a key Overweight. We trim our three- and 12-
month forecasts modestly, but see gold forming a strong base 
above USD 4,000/oz. Rising bond yields and the USD were 
recent headwinds, but we expect a softer USD and continued 
EM central bank demand to result in 12-month gains.

2 Foundation: Our tactical asset allocation

### Page 7

Global Market Outlook 7

Summary

Artificial intelligence (AI) has become one of the most powerful drivers of global market performance and, increasingly, 
one of the largest contributors to portfolio risk. While we remain constructive on the long-term outlook for equities and 
continue to view AI as a transformational investment theme, our tactical positioning has recently shifted from an Overweight to 
a Neutral equity weight. This reflects a more balanced near-term risk-reward profile after a period of strong equity market 
performance and increasingly concentrated market leadership. 
Against this backdrop, a deeper understanding of portfolio construction is essential. Investors should consider not only 
how much they allocate to equities, bonds and alternative investments, but also the underlying factors driving portfolio risk. Using 
a Total Portfolio Approach (TPA), we assessed the factor exposures embedded within our Foundation and Foundation+ 
Balanced portfolios, with a particular focus on AI concentration risk. 
Our analysis suggests both portfolios are more diversified than a traditional 60/40 allocation and maintain meaningful 
exposure to long-term AI growth opportunities. The Foundation+ Balanced portfolio provides additional diversification 
through alternative investments and a broader range of return drivers, reducing reliance on public equity markets.

Key findings

Looking beyond asset classes: Understanding 
AI concentration risk 
Investors often assess diversification through the lens of asset 
allocation. A portfolio containing equities, bonds, gold and 
alternatives may appear diversified on the surface. However, 
this traditional approach alone does not always reveal where 
portfolio risks are truly concentrated. 
TPA starts from a different premise. Rather than focusing solely 
on asset classes, it seeks to identify the underlying factors that 
drive portfolio outcomes, including equity markets, interest 
rates, credit spreads, currencies, liquidity conditions and 
thematic exposures such as AI. 
This perspective is particularly relevant following our recent 
move from an Overweight to a Neutral equity allocation. While 
we remain constructive on equities over the long term, a 
growing share of market returns has been driven by a relatively 
narrow group of AI-related beneficiaries. For investors, the 
question is, therefore, not whether to maintain exposure to AI, 
but whether their portfolios have become overly dependent on 
a single theme or underlying risk factor.

Asset Allocation insights: Assessing

### Page 8

Global Market Outlook 8

US equities exhibit some of the highest levels of AI intensity, 
reflecting the dominant role US companies play across the AI 
value chain. By contrast, fixed income, private assets and gold 
display significantly lower AI sensitivity and derive their 
returns from different economic drivers. 
The objective is not to eliminate AI exposure. We continue to 
believe AI will remain one of the defining structural growth 
themes of the coming decade. Instead, the objective is to 
ensure portfolios participate in this opportunity without 
becoming excessively dependent on it. 
Stronger diversification through different 
return drivers 
One of the most common misconceptions is that greater 
diversification inevitably leads to lower returns. Our analysis 
suggests the opposite may be true when diversification is 
achieved through genuinely different return drivers. Both 
Foundation and Foundation + portfolios exhibit attractive risk-
return characteristics relative to a traditional 60/40 allocation 
(see Fig. 9). They deliver comparable or higher return 
potential with lower volatility, smaller drawdowns and reduced 
sensitivity to global equity markets.

Analysis for the period of June 2016 to June 2026.

The key insight is that diversification works best when risks 
originate from different economic drivers. Simply increasing 
the number of securities or asset classes is not sufficient. 
What matters is the breadth of return sources supporting 
portfolio outcomes. 
AI remains a meaningful component of portfolio risk, and its 
exposure is spread across several parts of the ecosystem. 
Non-AI-related factors, including equity beta, credit, 
currencies and liquidity premia, also drive portfolio risk. This 
broader mix reduces dependence on any single investment 
theme and creates a more balanced portfolio structure. 
The role of alternatives: Creating additional 
return engines 
The most important distinction between our Foundation and 
Foundation+ portfolios is the inclusion of hedge funds and 
private assets in the latter. Alternatives can provide additional 
return engines and reduce reliance on equity market beta, but 
different strategies offer very different forms of diversification. 
Within hedge funds, a multi-strategy provides a strong 
starting point for investors seeking lower equity sensitivity and 
differentiated return sources, with an equity beta of around 
0.15-0.20 and a correlation slightly above 50% to global 
equities’ performance. Equity long/short retains greater 
participation in equity-led growth, reflected in a higher 0.45 
beta, and has historically captured 56% of global equity gains. 
However, it also maintains greater common exposure to 
equity and AI factors. Relative value offers the lowest 
equity exposure, with a beta of around 0.10 and participation 
below 5% of global equity losses, although nearly 80% of its 
modelled risk is attributed to credit (partly reflecting the use of 
convertible-arbitrage trades), highlighting that reducing equity 
beta can mean substituting it with another source of risk.  
Within private markets, the sources of return also differ 
materially. Private credit offers the most differentiated 
return engine, with returns driven primarily by income and 
credit premia rather than equity appreciation. Private equity, 
by contrast, remains more closely linked to equity and 
growth factors, making it a stronger source of long-term return 
potential but a less effective tool for reducing portfolio beta. 
Infrastructure can provide an attractive middle ground. It 
retains exposure to AI-related structural themes, particularly 
through digital and power infrastructure, and offers lower equity 
beta and correlation than traditional public equities. 
Ultimately, our move from an Overweight to a Core allocation 
in equity markets does not change our constructive long-term 
view on equities or AI. Rather, it reinforces the importance of 
understanding where portfolio risk originates. Investors do not 
need to choose between participating in the AI opportunity 
and maintaining diversification. Using a TPA lens combines 
exposure to long-term growth themes with a broader set of 
return drivers, including carefully selected alternatives, and 
reduces dependence on any single source of market 
performance.

### Page 9

Global Market Outlook 9

Key themes

Core scenario (soft landing, 45% probability): An economic soft landing remains our core scenario, with the global economy 
overcoming this year’s oil price shock thanks to a surge in AI investment in the US and Asia. Record wealth generation driven 
by the stock market rally is supporting US consumption. Fiscal easing in the US, Germany and Japan is supporting growth. 
Meanwhile, inflation has reaccelerated due to a rebound in fuel prices as the Middle East conflict drags on. Against this backdrop, 
the Fed, ECB and BoJ all hiked rates in the same month (in September) for the first time. Although all three subsequently paused 
in October, we expect the Fed to hike twice more, the ECB once more and the BoJ thrice more by June 2027. Nevertheless, we 
expect the energy and tariff effects on inflation to fade by H2 2027, enabling the Fed and the ECB to cut rates by Q3 2027. 
Meanwhile, China is likely to target credit easing and accelerate fiscal spending in H1 2027 to revive domestic growth.  
Upside risk (no landing, 30% probability): We maintain the probability at 30% as AI-driven investment growth continues. 
There is a reasonable chance that lower oil prices next year, the AI-fuelled wealth boom and fiscal easing in Germany and Japan 
will boost ‘animal spirits’. A potential global defence spending boom, led by the US and Europe, could also lift global growth. 
Downside risk (25% probability): We raised the risk of a recession to 15%, especially if central banks are forced to accelerate 
the pace of rate hikes to douse rising inflation. A stock market downturn negating the wealth effect or a bond sell-off due to fiscal 
concerns are other tail risks. We cut stagflation probability to 10% as we see low risk of oil prices sustaining above USD 120/bbl.

Policy rates watch  
We expect the Fed to hike twice more by Q2 2027, then 
ease in H2 2027, as AI-led expansion continues. The US 
economy remains firmly in expansion. In Q3, annualised GDP 
growth accelerated to 3.4%, led by AI investments and 
consumption. In October, the composite Purchasing 
Managers’ Index (PMI) remained strong at 57.6. Since our last 
monthly report, the consensus estimate for 2026 GDP growth 
has been nudged up to 2.3% from 2.1%, and the 2027 
estimate has risen to 2.2%. That matches the Fed’s upgraded 
growth projection of 2.3% for 2026, but remains below the 
Fed’s 2.4% estimate for 2027. AI investment, fiscal support 
and household wealth are the main growth drivers, although 
consumer activity lags business spending. Higher bond yields 
and elevated oil prices are headwinds, but recession risks 
look contained. Our core scenario remains a ‘soft landing’, at 
45%, with a meaningful 30% chance of a ‘no-landing’ upside. 
Rising gasoline and diesel prices pushed US core personal 
consumption expenditures (PCE) inflation to 0.3% m/m and 
3.1% y/y, matching estimates. Consensus sees core PCE 
inflation slowing from 3.2% in Q4 2026 to 2.4% by Q4 2027 
as energy and tariff effects fade. Underlying price momentum 
is softening, and recent PCE methodology revisions have 
lowered core inflation. However, fuel costs passed on to wider 
consumers and AI-driven chip shortages remain upside risks. 
In October, the Fed held rates at 4.0%, after hiking in 
September for the first time since 2023. We expect the Fed to 
hike rates by 25bps in December and 25bps more in H1 2027 
due to elevated near-term energy inflation. It is likely to follow 
with a 25bps cut to 4.25% in Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This aligns with the Fed’s projected policy path, although it 
implies a more dovish trajectory than money markets, which 
are pricing another 56bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
Political uncertainty after the mid-term election is a key risk.

6 Macro overview – at a glance

Rajat Bhattacharya 
Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 10

Global Market Outlook 10 
ECB to hike once more in December, then pause. The 
Euro area recovery is firming. GDP grew 1.2% y/y in Q2 2026, 
up from 0.6% in Q1, and the manufacturing PMI stayed robust 
at 52.4 in October. Consensus 2026 growth has been raised 
to 1.0% from 0.9%, and 2027 is unchanged at 1.3%. The main 
supports are resilient exports and German infrastructure and 
defence spending. However, cautious consumers and soft 
private investment limit the recovery, and energy costs are 
squeezing household incomes and business margins. 
Recession is not the central expectation. French fiscal stress 
and sustained high energy costs remain the key risks. 
Higher energy prices pushed Euro area headline inflation up 
to 3.8% y/y in September from 3.2% y/y in August, while core 
inflation rose to 2.5% y/y. Consensus expects headline and 
core inflation to ease to 2% y/y and 2.4% y/y by Q4 2027, 
respectively. Encouragingly, energy has had limited impact on 
core prices, and longer-term inflation expectations remain 
stable at around 2.1%. Together, these should allow inflation 
to moderate in 2027. The main risk is that energy, and 
especially gas, prices stay high for longer than expected. 
The ECB held its deposit rate at 2.5% in October after raising 
it by 50bps this year. Inflation data continue to favour an 
additional hike in December. We expect an ECB rate hold in 
H1 2027, followed by a cut by Q3 2027 as disinflation returns. 
This policy path is more dovish than money markets, which 
are pricing more than 60bps of hikes over the next 12 months. 
China to provide targeted support to defend growth floor. 
China's growth momentum recovered in September. Both the 
manufacturing PMI (50.1) and the non-manufacturing PMI 
(50.2) moved back into expansion. The construction PMI rose 
above 50 for the first time this year as infrastructure spending 
started to take effect. Exports grew 36.3% y/y, pushing the 
trade surplus to a record USD 133.5bn. We expect growth in 
2026 to meet the lower end of the 4.5-5.0% target. Growth is 
increasingly bifurcated, though, with technology firms and 
exporters doing well, while household demand stays weak. 
Prices are rising again, but only narrowly. Consumer inflation 
edged up to 0.9% y/y in September, and producer inflation 
surged to 5% y/y, its highest since 2022, driven by energy and 
metals prices and concentrated in upstream sectors. With 
domestic demand weak and a high comparison base next 
year, the boost from energy prices is likely to fade in 2027. 
Policy is in ‘targeted easing’ mode. We expect a modest fiscal 
package to defend the growth floor rather than a large 
stimulus. The PBoC cut a key rate by 25bps to 1.5% to fund 
‘Six Networks’ infrastructure projects and raised its relending 
quotas. A new interest subsidy on first-home mortgages 
provides limited support to demand. We expect no policy rate 
cut this year. There is still room to speed up fiscal spending, 
including by using CNY 500bn of unused local-government 
debt quota. If the PBoC switches to an overnight reverse repo 
rate as its policy rate, that would imply a de facto rate cut as 
the overnight rate is lower than the 7-day official policy rate. 
BoJ to hike at a quarterly pace until Q2 2027. Japan's 
economy remains solid. Wages are growing above 4%, and 
the labour market is tight. The main headwind is high energy 
prices, which is slowing growth. Fiscal spending gives some 
support: the FY27 draft budget includes ambitious spending 
plans, alongside a proposed two-year cut to the consumption 
tax on food and a new five-year defence programme. 
However, this extra spending raises questions about fiscal 
sustainability. The BoJ unveiled plans to triple the pace of ETF 
sales from January 2027, potentially funding the tax cut. 
Price pressures now look firmly established. Headline and 
core inflation rose above 2% for the first time this year, and 
core-core inflation (ex-fresh food and energy) jumped to 2.4% 
and is expected to rise towards 3% in Q1 2027. Breakeven 
inflation rates have stayed above 2% most of this year. Real 
interest rates remain negative, hurting savers and pensioners. 
The BoJ held its policy rate at 1.25% in October, after raising 
it in September, only three months after its previous hike. This 
points to an accelerated quarterly hiking pace. We expect the 
quarterly pace of hikes to continue, taking the rate to 2% by 
June 2027. Money markets have caught up with our hawkish 
view in recent months. The BoJ noted for the first time that 
underlying inflation has reached its 2% target, marking a shift 
from creating inflation to containing it. Politics could slow the 
hiking cycle in H2 2027. Two government-appointed board 
members voted against the September hike, and the two most 
hawkish members are due to leave in July 2027.

### Page 11

Global Market Outlook 11

Our view

Foundation: Bond yields remain near multi-year highs, offering an attractive entry point. We view fixed income as a Core holding 
(ie, a Neutral allocation) in our multi-asset portfolios. Within the asset class, we maintain an Overweight stance on EM USD 
government bonds, given their attractive risk-reward. We hold an Underweight view on Developed Market (DM) government 
bonds due to the tail risks associated with term premia. 
The Fed’s decision to hold rates at 3.75-4.00% in October amid still-elevated energy prices suggests that every Fed meeting 
from here is ‘live’, with a rate change possible on incoming data. With US inflation still running hot and the labour market tight, 
we expect two more 25bps hikes (one by year-end and one in H1 2027), bringing the Fed’s target range for the federal funds 
rate to 4.25-4.5%. We continue to favour the 3-7-year part of the US government bond yield curve as the best hedge against 
any risk-off move driven by AI-related growth fears. As inflation pressures ease in H2 2027, we see room for the Fed to consider 
a rate cut, which should pull nominal yields lower. Solid corporate fundamentals and supportive technicals should keep spreads 
tight, but there is limited room for them to tighten further. We still favour EM USD over local-currency (LCY) government bonds. 
Opportunistic ideas: We are bullish on US TIPS, AAA-rated CLOs, broad US HYs and Australian dollar (AUD) corporate bonds.

DM rates – Underweight 
We remain Underweight DM government bonds. In the US, 
government bond yields have retraced meaningfully after a 
sharp bear steepening through September and early October. 
We expect real yields to stay elevated over the next six 
months as AI capex and productivity growth continue to drive 
above-trend economic growth. We expect two more 25bps 
rate hikes by June 2027.  
Over the next few months, we expect US government bond 
yields to remain rangebound. The near-term path hinges on 
five factors: 1) the 3 November US mid-term election, where 
a ‘Democratic sweep’ would likely be bond-supportive by 
constraining US President Trump’s fiscal spending agenda, 
while a ‘Republican sweep’ would likely weigh on bonds as 
markets price in further fiscal expansion and a higher term 
premium; 2) Fed communication at upcoming policy meetings; 
3) the US Treasury’s quarterly refunding announcement on 4 
November, where any signal of reduced long-end issuance 
would likely compress long-end yields; 4) softening auction 
demand and repatriation by foreign investors, which add 
uncertainty around demand for US government bonds; and 5) 
the fluid geopolitical situation in the Middle East, which is 
keeping oil prices elevated, with a risk of further upside. 
Going into H2 2027, we expect moderating oil prices and the 
fading inflationary impact of tariffs to give the Fed room to 
consider rate cuts. The US 30-year yield, however, is likely to 
stay elevated as fiscal deficit remains substantial next year.  
We expect a final ECB hike to 2.75% by December 2026, then 
a pause in H1 2027, followed by a 25bps cut in Q3 2027. In 
Japan, we expect quarterly pace of BoJ hikes, with one hike 
that takes the policy rate to 1.50% by December 2026 and two 
more in H1 2027, reaching 2.00% before pausing at mid-year.

7 Fixed Income – at a glance

Jonathan Liang, CFA 
CIO, Fixed Income & FX 
Cedric Lam 
Senior Investment Strategist 
Ray Heung

Anthony Naab, CFA 
Investment Strategist

### Page 12

Global Market Outlook 12 
DM corporates – Core holding 
We maintain a Neutral allocation to both DM IG and DM HY 
corporate bonds, with a tactical bias to add exposure on 
episodic weakness, as carry continues to dominate total 
returns across DM credit. Credit spreads have widened from 
their lows but remain close to historical tights, while yields are 
still attractive, reinforcing a carry-led stance. This tight-spread 
regime is anchored by robust issuer fundamentals, strong 
profitability and resilient balance sheets, despite a visible 
moderation in rating upgrades over the past quarter.  
Technical supply dynamics are reshaping sector dispersion, 
especially in the IG space, where heavy AI-related issuance 
is weighing on the long end of the curve. AI-driven supply is 
surpassing USD 250bn in 2026 and making up a growing 
share of US IG gross issuance, but there are growing signs 
that hyperscalers are turning to private markets for funding, 
which should help ease public bond market supply pressures.  
We remain selective at the long end of the yield curve and 
favour a duration of 3-7 years, where risk-adjusted carry 
remains most durable. Issuance-driven concessions in 
sectors with strong capex cycles (notably technology, capital 
goods and basic industries) present opportunities for investors 
who can stomach near-term volatility. 
Fig. 14 DM IG spreads have widened from their lows, 
but remain near historically tight levels 
Bloomberg Global Aggregate Corp Index, option-adjusted 
spread (OAS)

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
DM HY spreads have widened to roughly a six-month high. 
Persistent doubts about whether hyperscalers can generate 
adequate returns on massive AI capex investments triggered 
a broad risk asset sell-off. The CCC tier has become the focal 
point of genuine credit concern, with yields hovering near the 
high-15% range and spreads near a four-year high. Market 
focus has shifted to the funding pressures facing AI-related 
neocloud companies. We view this as idiosyncratic for now 
and see a limited impact on the broader HY universe. The 
underlying credit picture is mixed. Leverage is elevated and 
interest coverage is thin, but the broad market has not yet 
shown signs of systemic stress. The US HY default rate 
remains below trend in 2026. 
HY offers an attractive income proposition, with all-in yields of 
over 8%. Its shorter-duration profile, combined with its lower 
sensitivity to changes in the term premium and rate volatility, 
make it an attractive sleeve in the current phase of the cycle. 
We remain attentive to evolving credit rating migration trends 
and sector-specific supply pressures, particularly as upgrades 
slow and new issuance clusters around key themes. However, 
healthy issuer fundamentals and stable credit metrics 
continue to support it as a Core holding allocation. 
Fig. 15 DM HY spreads are off the tights 
Bloomberg Global HY Aggregate Corporate Index, OAS

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered 
EM government bonds – Overweight 
USD/Core holding for LCY 
We are Overweight EM USD government bonds and retain 
EM LCY government bonds as a Core holding, reflecting a 
balanced perspective across both regional performance and 
currency dynamics. In EM USD sovereign debt, the recent 
spread widening offered an attractive entry point to gain 
exposure to areas benefiting from resilient global growth and 
stable external conditions. Technicals remain favourable, with 
supply being front-loaded and investor positioning remaining 
relatively light compared with historical levels. 
Many EM central banks having tightened earlier and more 
aggressively post-pandemic, preserving credibility and 
maintaining positive real rates. External balances have 
improved, reserve buffers have increased and greater 
reliance on domestic funding reduces external vulnerability. 
Lula’s surprise win in Brazil’s presidential election raises 
political risk. The country’s fiscal trajectory under the incoming 
administration is a key risk to monitor. Fiscal slippage could 
trigger a broader repricing of LATAM risk. In Asia, higher-rated 
issuers continue to offer a valuable buffer during bouts of 
global rate volatility, while select frontier exposures in EMEA 
remain more sensitive to liquidity shifts and changes in risk 
appetite. Index composition also shapes resilience: the EM 
USD benchmark is less exposed to oil price shocks, as 
countries that are not oil importers account for a larger share 
of the benchmark than in the LCY benchmark. We expect 
returns to be primarily driven by carry rather than further 
spread compression.
